ParlaMint-PL_2021-10-01-sejm-38-3.u5	Dear Marshal, Dear Minister, High Izbo! I have the great honour to present the position of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice on the government draft law amending certain laws in order to facilitate the conduct of agricultural retail trade by farmers, print No 1516. The contested bill is primarily the implementation of an initiative named: the release of agricultural retail trade contained in the government document of Poland. The Law and Justice Government is therefore taking another step towards making even better use of the 2017 tool dedicated to farmers, a tool which is very popular among them, as evidenced by the fact that in our country today, this form of food sales is being carried out by several thousand farmers. The draft law proposes amendments to the Food Safety and Nutrition Act and the Personal Income Tax Act, which aim, first, to enlarge the area in which supplies of food produced in the framework of agricultural retail trade can be carried out to retail establishments for the final consumer, such as. restaurants, cafés, shops - to the territory of the whole country. Currently, this area includes the voivodship in which food production is carried out within the RHD, as well as districts or cities established in the voivodship or seymous of neighbouring voivodships with this voivodship, while the sale of final consumers within the framework of this activity will be possible as to date throughout the country. This will enable farmers to expand the market for food produced while meeting food safety requirements. Secondly, the draft law proposes the abolition of the maximum limits for food sold in the framework of the agricultural retail trade to final consumers and leaving these limits only for supplies to retail establishments for final consumers. This option will enable farmers to increase their sales of food to consumers, reduce the administrative burden of keeping records, and also allow farmers to make better use of the possibilities of tax exemptions introduced by the proposed law. Thirdly, the draft law provides for an additional exemption of the producer from personal income tax, i.e. increase from 40,000. Up to 100,000. zł amounts of revenue from sales processed in a manner other than industrial plant and animal products, except processed plant and animal products obtained within the framework of special agricultural production divisions and products subject to excise duty on the basis of separate provisions exempt from income tax. The adoption of such a solution will reduce the number of persons required to make a written statement on the choice of flat-rate taxation from the revenues recorded for the tax year in question and, on the other hand, will enable agricultural producers to obtain higher income from sales of processed products other than industrial plant and animal products. In conclusion, the proposed changes should result in increased opportunities for farmers to develop their agricultural retail business by extending the range of food consumers, e.g. shops, restaurants, cafés and other such establishments from all over the country, and the introduction of unlimited food sales to consumers, and this should contribute to an increase in the incomes of agricultural producers. This draft law is subject to the procedure of notification to the European Commission and the Member States of the European Union, and therefore ultimately the scope of the proposed amendments will also depend on the opinion of the European Commission and the Member States of the European Union notified under this procedure. The draft was notified for notification on 5 August 2021 and the mandatory deadline for deferring the adoption of the notified project set by the European Commission is 8 November 2021. Dear Marshal, Dear Minister, High Izbo! I would like to inform you that the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice will support the Government's bill to amend certain laws (the Bell) in order to make it easier for farmers to carry out their agricultural retail trade in the course of parliamentary work. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2021-10-01-sejm-38-3.u9	Dear Marshal, Dear Minister, High Izbo! I have the honour to present the position of the Left parliamentary club vis-à-vis the government draft law amending certain laws in order to facilitate the conduct of the agricultural retail trade by farmers, print No 1516. First, let me briefly explain what the agricultural retail trade is, in short, the RHD. It is a specific form of retail trade dedicated to farmers, which has been operating in Poland since 2017. The basic condition for RHD is that the food must come wholly or partly from its own cultivation, breeding or farming. The agricultural retail trade is favourable due to tax exemptions. At present, farmers are exempt from income tax up to 40 000. zł. It is possible to produce, including small-scale food processing, and to dispose of it to final consumers, as well as to local retail establishments for final consumer. In the context of agricultural retail trade, raw materials of plant origin may be sold, e.g. cereals, vegetables, fruit, herbs and animals, e.g. milk, cream, eggs, honey, but also processed food, e.g. oils, breads, jams, juices, preparations, as well as the sale and preparation of meals such as dumplings, hooves, Silesian noodles, dumplings, etc., etc. This draft law contains some key changes in the functioning of agricultural retail trade. Firstly, the draft law proposes the complete abolition of the maximum food limits for the sale of final consumers and leaving only those limits which relate to supplies to retail establishments, e.g. to shops, restaurants or cafés. The second amendment consists of an increase in the cap on sales income exempt from personal income tax: from 40 000. Up to 100,000. zł. Another change is to increase the area where food supplies can be made to retail establishments, such as shops, restaurants and cafés, to the territory of the country as a whole. Currently, this area is limited to voivodship and powiats or cities adjacent to voivodship. The last amendment is to change the definition of the concept of agricultural retail trade. If the product consists of one ingredient, the definition remains unchanged. The change occurs in the case of foods containing more than one ingredient, e.g. processed meat products, dairy products, but also prepared meals. The proposed amendment is that food should contain at least one ingredient wholly derived from its own cultivation, breeding or rearing. As a general rule, the idea of simplifying the sale of agricultural foetuses is needed. We should strive to reduce supply chains and to ensure that farmers achieve higher wages for their work, capital and risks. This direction is in line with the European Green Deal policy, from field to table, although it should be noted that EU countries are proposing different solutions. However, I would like to point out that the amendments proposed in the Act also involve certain risks. The first concerns state finances, e.g. The abolition of maximum food limits may give rise to the risk that some foods will be sold unfairly, without tax, not only to final consumers, but also to shops, restaurants and cafés. The revision of the definition of agricultural retail trade also seems too far-reaching. What, for example? If there are only Polish radishes in the prepared meal, and the rest are rice from China, soy from the USA, meat and tomatoes from Spain? According to this definition, this meal can be sold without tax. I hope that bill wasn't written under anyone. I want to assume the applicant's good intentions and hope to improve it in the legislative process. At the same time, I ask the Minister to make a fair comparison on what legal solutions exist in other countries of the European Union so that we can make a good decision at the committee meeting. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2021-10-01-sejm-38-3.u63	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! I have the honour to present to you a draft amendment to the Commercial Companies Code and some other laws. This is the largest reform of the Commercial Companies Code for 20 years, prepared by the State Commission for State Assets Acting at the Ministry of Finance. Reforms of Ownership Supervision. This work was conducted in broad consultation and many discussions. In order to encourage you to work together on transforming this project into part of the Polish legal order, I would like to give you an insight into the reasons why this draft reform of the Commercial Companies Code was created. I will also present the silhouettes of people who were involved in its preparation. I will talk about the broad consultation that has been followed by the preparation of the project, which is the subject of our deliberations today. I will also give you an overview of the most important instruments that have been included in the project. But let's start with why the Ministry of State Assets started working on the code. This is a direct effect of talking to entrepreneurs and signals that they expect change. So the project I present is primarily a response to the needs reported by the Polish business. But the need for change is also due to the success of our economy, how much our economy has developed in recent years. In place of 205 grand. companies that operated in 2000, today we have 460 thousand. companies, and entities that have only taken first steps in business in 2000, are sometimes managing dozens of subsidiaries and trading billions of zlotys. A more complex economy requires a law tailored to its needs. Besides, this development of the Polish economy, Polish companies are still progressing, of which I hope the entire High Chamber is proud. Our country also competes with other countries more and more courageously and dynamically, so it needs the right tools to do so. Good economic law is a way for Poland to win this competition as often as possible. Such conditions create this project, gives advanced tools for a good competitive fight in very demanding foreign markets. Importantly, the consequence of our country's entry into the European Union is that our country competes with other countries as a place of business placement. Good economic law is a way for Poland to win this competition as often as possible. In order for the Commercial Companies Code to meet its requirements, we invited recognised experts to cooperate with a wide range of practical knowledge. The teams that prepared the project that we are presenting to the High House today were headed by Prof. Andrzej Szumański from the Jagiellonian University, who is one of three authors of the Commercial Companies Code, Dr. Radosław Kwaśnicki, a recognized corporate governance expert, and Prof. Piotr Pinior from the University of Silesia, also a leading commercial law expert. I would also like to highlight the very important role, the huge work done on this project by the current director, PhD Filip Ostrowski, [[Applause]] Head of the Owner's Reform Office at the Ministry of State Assets. The bill is therefore intended to open up new opportunities for our entities, to give our companies new tools. And the great advantage of this project is that not only scientists, but also entrepreneurs participated in its preparation from the very beginning. So we are not dealing with a project that has only been consulted with business, but we are dealing with a project whose business is a co-author. The consultation was also very broad around the project. We have invited more than 200 actors to present their positions. We've extended the consultation time to 45 days. We have received more than 800 pages of comments, and in many cases we have taken these comments into account in the proposal that we are submitting to the High House today. Mr Janusz Kowalski was responsible for this area for the first part of his work on the project, including the organizational project, in the Ministry of State Assets. [[Applause]] Thank you very much from this place today. At the head of the Committee on the Functioning of the European Union, The Owner's Reform stands Deputy Prime Minister, Minister of Assets Mr Jacek Sasin. [[Applause]] [[Voice from the room: Another.]] [[Voice from the room: Expert.]] The flagship part of the project is the regulation of groups of companies to give parent companies the opportunity to effectively manage subsidiaries while safeguarding the interests of other economic players. The current provisions of Article. 7 Commercial Companies Code does not meet any of these objectives. Sometimes, in doctrine, one can see the view that problems in this area will be solved by the judiciary, but the Polish legal system does not belong to the systems of the countries of customary law. The responsibility for lawmaking and the social mandate to do so lies with the High House. After the entry into force of the new rules, parent companies will be given the right to issue binding orders to subsidiaries. They will be able to obtain information from their subsidiaries on a special basis. They will also be able to buy out minority shareholders. With the additional powers of the parent company, it corresponds to its additional liability to creditors and minority shareholders. The interests of these two groups have been secured by formalism to accompany the issuing of binding orders. On the other hand, the counterpart of the parent company's right of repurchase is granted to minority shareholders. Solutions to improve the efficiency of the supervisory board are a response to the reported problems that many times after irregularities occurred in a company, problems arose later. Where was the board of directors? Therefore, the proposed changes address fundamental issues for corporate governance, how to ensure that the supervisory board has information about what is happening in the company and is able to process this information. After the provisions proposed by us have come into force, this will be the case. To the rest of the project included provisions clarifying the fundamental responsibilities of members of boards and supervisory boards: secrecy obligations, loyalty obligations. The principle of business assessment has also been codified in many European countries. One of the fathers of Polish trade law Aleksander Doliński wrote: The lawyer gives only form, content is provided by the economist. This is how this ambitious project was created in relation to the best traditions of Polish economic law. The contents were delivered by the business. Prominent lawyers gave form. And now all we have to do is ask the High House to work on this project in substance and substance. The tradition of legislation in the area of trade law is that even where people shared political differences, and these also divide us today, we can create a good law for the Polish economy, because differences shared the authors of the 1934 Commercial Code and shared this High Chamber, which in 2000 adopted the Commercial Companies Code. In both cases, however, it was possible to work together over divisions in order to be very important and aimed at public benefit. This is the kind of cooperation I encourage and invite colleagues from the High Chamber. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2021-10-01-sejm-38-3.u77	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The proposed provisions regulate the relationship between the parent company and its subsidiaries and equip supervisory boards with tools for more effective corporate supervision. Regulation introduced in 2000 of the holding law repealing Article. 7 of the Commercial Companies Code did not take into account the regulation of the legal issues of factual holding companies, i.e. companies between which the relationship of dominance and dependency arose. New chapter titled: The group of companies concerns real-time holding companies and responds to business requests. The economic environment is divided into supporters and opponents of the granting of wide powers to the parent company's authorities vis-à-vis companies belonging to the group of companies. The creation of a holding structure does not mean the exclusion of tax law which imposes sanctions on the transfer of profits, in particular for breach of the principle of concluding contracts between affiliated companies on a market-based basis under the same conditions as between independent entities. The definition of a group of companies is that companies participating in the group adopt a common economic strategy which indicates that the parent company exercises uniform management over subsidiaries. This is equivalent to the acceptance of the functional criterion. As near Kukiz15 - Direct Democracy, we consider the proposed regulations to be necessary for the smooth management of businesses. That is why we are in favour of further proceedings and we are counting on the swift adoption of the law. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2021-10-01-sejm-38-3.u116	Thank you very much, senator. If the senator wishes to stay on this point, then it will be possible to refer to the speeches and questions later. ParlaMint-PL_2021-10-01-sejm-38-3.u117	Mr. Marshal! Minister! Mr. Senator! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I have the honour to present an opinion on the Senate draft law amending the law - Code of Civil Procedure, print No 1225. The draft law provides for the transfer of the arrangements contained in the Regulation of the Minister of Justice of 5 July 2017 on the identification of the items belonging to the farmer holding the holding which are not subject to enforcement to the provisions of the Code of Civil Procedure. This project is inspired by the request contained in the individual petition to the Senate, in which attention was drawn, among others. to the fact that, in the light of constitutional requirements, any exemption from enforcement relating to animals and movable goods necessary for the running of an agricultural holding must be regulated at the statutory level. On the other hand, at the level of the implementing act, matters are currently regulated which affect both the right to courts - including its aspect, which involves guaranteeing the enforcement of a final judgment - and the protection of the creditor's property rights, which decides to initiate judicial execution against the farmer holding the agricultural holding. In addition, part of the provisions of the Regulation of 5 July 2017 go beyond the delegation of Article. 830 Civil Procedure Code. Following the proposed amendment, the rules on animals and objects belonging to a farmer holding a non-executable holding will regain the merit of constitutionality, more specifically: they will be structured according to the requirements of the standards set out in Article. 92. 1 and Article 4 (1) (b) of Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council [1] shall apply mutatis mutandis. Article 31 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 3 in conjunction with Article. Article 45 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 and Article 1 Article 64 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1st Constitution. The aim is also to increase the coherence of the legal system, as in the case of enforcement proceedings in the administration, the legislator has already identified those animals and movable goods which are excluded from enforcement if they are directed against the farmer holding the agricultural holding. The concept of statutory regulation of items belonging to the holding farmer which are not subject to enforcement and the rules governing the handling of such matters by the competent authorities should be assessed positively. This will undoubtedly increase the legal protection of farmers' debtors. However, the following issues should be considered for the project: the need to extend the deadline provided for in the proposed Article. 829 2 § 2 for the opinion of the Agricultural Chamber from 14 days to e.g. 21 or 30 days from the date of service of the summons and the appropriateness of requiring the bailiff to join the request for such an opinion of the minutes of seizure of the debtor's assets, together with information on the total amount of the debtor's claim, and the legal nature of the abovementioned opinions in the act and the possibility of verifying them by allowing evidence from the expert's opinion. The conclusion is that the adoption of the project in its current form will result in a reduction in the creditworthiness of farmers. Deficiencies of the existing § 5 of the Regulation is necessary simultaneously to direct executions to all immovable property where executions from one immovable property would allow the creditor to be satisfied. The aim should therefore be to extend the possibility of execution of property belonging to an agricultural holding where the creditor has a mortgage established for him by the debtor himself, or as soon as the execution from the real estate allows the creditor to be satisfied. A certain model for the protection of the debtor could be the solution adopted in Article. 952 1 § 1 and 4 of the Civil Procedure Code. Such solutions would make it possible to balance the interests of creditors and debtors without risking individual farmers benefiting from loans or bank loans at the same time. High Izbo! Taking into account the objections raised, the Law and Justice Club welcomes the draft law on the amendment of the Civil Procedure Code, print No 1225, and requests further work on it. Thank you.
ParlaMint-PL_2021-10-01-sejm-38-3.u118	Thank you very much, sir. Now I ask Mrs Joanna Jaśkowiak to present the position of the Civic Coalition Parliamentary Club.
ParlaMint-PL_2021-10-01-sejm-38-3.u119	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Mr. Senator! I would like to present the position of the Parliamentary Club Civil Coalition regarding the Senate draft law amending the law - Code of Civil Procedure, contained in print No 1225. I would like to stress that the proposed changes are inspired by a petition submitted to the Senate last year. According to the applicant, the identification of the animals and objects belonging to the farmer who runs the holding and which are not subject to enforcement constitutes statutory matter. To date, the issue of exclusion from enforcement of certain items has been governed by the provisions of the Regulation of the Minister of Justice of 5 July 2017 on the identification of items belonging to the farmer holding which are not subject to enforcement. It should be accepted that, in the light of constitutional requirements, any exemption from enforcement, both in respect of animals and moving goods needed to run an agricultural holding, must be regulated at the statutory level. There is no doubt, therefore, that the regulation is a normative act in the lower order, which can only be used for the implementation of the law, and cannot replace it, supplement it, or modify the solutions adopted. Therefore, following the adoption of the proposed amendment, the rules on animals and objects belonging to a farmer holding a non-executable holding will have the merit of constitutionality. The content of the proposed amendments almost faithfully reflects the provisions resulting from the above-mentioned Regulation of the Minister of Justice. As regards corrections, they concern rather editorial changes or the need to adjust references or terminology. This proposal also provides for the repeal of the current Article. 830 Code of Civil Procedure because all regulations will be governed by the law - those which were regulated by the Regulation - and it will become simply redundant. It is also important to introduce inter-temporal rules, as the existing rules will apply to cases initiated under the previous rules. So that the Civic Coalition Parliamentary Club very much asks for this project to be passed on in the form that has been sent through the Senate to further process, to carry out further work on it. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2021-10-01-sejm-38-3.u121	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Left Club, I submit my position on the resolution of the Senate amending the Civil Procedure Code Act. Following the entry of the bailiff into his holding because the farmer had ceased to pay his obligations because of not the cases he was responsible for, but because of drought or other crop failure on the holding or area concerned, the sale of these purchased machinery was not sufficient to repay the loans. What happened on these farms? You have certainly seen many times in TV broadcasts, how the bailiffs acted in the execution of agricultural obligations. There was no malice on the part of the farmer here, but merely the impossibility of paying back the commitments that were before him. High Izbo! An example from Wielkopolska, near my parliamentary office. The farmer took credit for the purchase of agricultural machinery. After the agency's enforcement of the debt, the bailiff led to the fact that this farm no longer exists today. It is not enough that the bailiff sold the machines and the inventory, yet - because it did not have enough money - he was about to take up agricultural land and also sold agricultural land. Unfortunately, at the time he was selling, the land on the market was actually very cheap. The fact that the bill that the Senate project crosses this situation, that we are moving the regulation of the Minister of Finance and the Minister of Agriculture to the Act, means that this idea is going in a very good direction and perhaps in a moment it will counteract those practices that have been applied until now. High Izbo! The left goes against these expectations and we will support the bill presented, but it would be worthwhile, Mr. Marshal, to discuss it more. ParlaMint-PL_2021-10-01-sejm-38-3.u122	Thank you very much, sir. There is a co-author of this bill, Senator Bober, who will certainly address all the issues. And now Mr Zbigniew Ziejewski will present the position of the Parliamentary Club of Poland.
ParlaMint-PL_2021-10-01-sejm-38-3.u123	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! Mr. Senator! I have the pleasure and the honour to present the position of the Parliamentary Club Coalition Poland - Polish People's Party, Union of European Democrats, Conservatives on the Senate bill amending the Act - Code of Civil Procedure, print No 1225. The project under investigation concerns the identification of animals and objects belonging to the farmer holding which are not subject to enforcement - so far it has been governed by the provisions of the Regulation of the Minister of Justice of 5 July 2017 on the identification of objects belonging to the farmer holding which are not subject to enforcement - and on the repeal of Article. 830 Law of 17 November 1964 - Code of Civil Procedure, as this provision has become redundant. It must be accepted that, in the light of constitutional requirements, any exemption from enforcement of animals and movable goods needed to run a farm must be regulated at the statutory level. They are currently regulated by the Regulation of the Minister of Justice on the identification of items belonging to the farmer holding which are not subject to enforcement. The regulation is a normative act which can only serve the enforcement of the law. It must not replace, supplement or modify the solutions adopted in it. Therefore, the proposed amendments are justified. The rich case law of the Constitutional Tribunal clearly clarifies the relationship between the law and the regulation. It was rightly noted that Art. 92. 1 of the Constitution provides that the authorisation to issue a regulation is to be determined by the competent authority. The degree of precision required by the basic law therefore excludes the so-called. Blanket authorizations are also incomplete. The provisions authorising this type of defect, in particular those which affect the requirement of detail in terms of the substance or content, do not satisfy the constitutional model referred to above. In accordance with the Article cited above. 830 The Civil Code of Procedure of the Minister of Justice, in agreement with the Minister for Agriculture and the Minister for Finance, is required to specify in the regulation which items belonging to the farmer holding the holding are not subject to enforcement. This authorisation does not merely contain any guidance on the content of the implementing act, it refers to matters that affect both the right to court in that aspect of it, which involves guaranteeing the enforcement of a final judgment and the protection of the creditor's property rights, which decides to initiate judicial execution against the farmer holding the agricultural holding. It is also important to note that some of the provisions of the Regulation of 5 July 2017 go beyond that delegation. In conclusion, the transfer to the Law of Solutions which have so far been included in the Regulation of the Minister of Justice of 5 July 2017 on the identification of items belonging to a farmer holding a holding which is not subject to enforcement, which contains almost the provisions contained in the Regulation, in addition to terminology or editorial corrections, is considered reasonable and necessary in view of the need to ensure the transparency and regularity of the position of legal standards in this regard. In addition, we agree with the extension of the deadline for issuing by the competent farmhouse, as mentioned by the senator's colleague, an opinion on the appropriateness of excluding from enforcement surplus livestock or items in more than the provisions of the Article. 829 Code of Civil Procedure, from 14 days to 21 days and the elimination of (the bell) of the regulation providing that the provisions governing the exemption from enforcement shall not apply if the execution is simultaneously directed to all property belonging to the holding operated by the debtor. At this point, I would like to appeal to all farmers who are in debt for ASF and avian influenza to benefit from this article, which was prepared by the Senate, in print No.1225, and to get acquainted with it - it is what the bailiff cannot take away from them - and they turned to the agricultural chambers, because it is the agricultural chambers that are consulting these proposals. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

The second timespace relates to the period after January 2023. According to the submitted project, the permit will be available to the university, which, according to the date of submission of the application, has a scientific category A+, A or B+ in the discipline of medical science or health science or conducts studies in at least one course in preparation for the professions referred to in Article. 68, and has minimal scientific category C in medical science or in health science. In addition to the financial support of students taking up and taking up these studies, the proposed solution will help to reduce the phenomenon of emigration of Polish university graduates who are doctors and increase the number of specialist doctors, in particular in the areas of medicine considered as priority. In addition, the introduction of this support will be an opportunity for qualified persons to acquire a doctor's profession, who, despite having obtained good results on the maturity exam, did not get into a fixed-mode medical course and which, among other things, did not. because of the lack of sufficient financial resources, they could not undertake these studies for payment. When such students benefit from a medical degree loan, it will be possible even to cancel the loan completely if the doctor fulfils the following cumulative conditions, which the Minister also referred to: the obligation to study after they have completed for a period of not less than 10 years over 12 consecutive years from the date of completion of their studies and the obligation to obtain a specialist title during that period considered as a priority on the day on which the doctor started specialization training. Other important changes in the bill are the differentiation of the period of student benefits depending on the duration of the studies, as well as the clarification of the way in which this period is determined, the attractiveness of the student credit system for creditors, the improvement of the process of verification of student and doctoral status during student credit and medical college loans based on the POL-on system. Designed solutions aimed at financial support for students, increasing the number of universities conducting medical and dental studies, and thus the number of students, reducing the phenomenon of emigration of Polish university graduates who are doctors and increasing the number of specialist doctors, in particular in the areas of medicine considered as priority, we consider it good, mitigating in the perspective of the problems of our health system. Therefore, the Law and Justice Club supports the proposed project. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2021-10-01-sejm-38-3.u145	Mr. Marshal! Gentlemen of Ministers! High Izbo! The bill submitted is an attempt to correct the poor situation in healthcare. One of the causes of this condition, as we know, is the dramatic shortage of doctors. Poland is at the very end of OECD countries in terms of the number of doctors. We've got 2.4 doctors out of 1,000. population, while the average in OECD countries is 3.5. The reason is partly for emigration of medics. Currently, one third of the residents are considering emigration from Poland. According to the authors of the bill themselves, during the PiS regime, from 2015 to 2019 the total number of doctors educated in our country whose professional qualifications were recognised in the European Union and Norway increased by around 63%. After the opinion of the LEP, most doctors receive a job offer in EU, UK or Norway. They are offered better wages, easier access to specialisation, better social conditions, respect, protection. They're well-educated, they speak languages, so they're leaving. We have one of the lowest employment rates in the healthcare sector in Europe. Over 10 years, between 2011 and 2020 the evaluation of medical procedures increased by only 11%. which makes hospitals already indebted to about PLN 18 billion. Medical rescue is breaking down. Poorly paid young doctors are burdened with very many on-call calls. Health care should be an absolute priority in state spending in the coming years. That's not what's happening. The evolution of the share of GDP in healthcare expenditure is far too slow. Globally, healthcare expenditure is increased by around 5% each year. which causes, that despite a little more expenditure on health in Poland, developed countries escape us even more. The authors of the law want to increase the number of medical students while trying to stop doctors in Poland. They propose: to increase the number of universities that could open up studies in medical sciences and dentistry; to encourage young people to study medicine, which is to be used by the new system of scholarships and loans; to stop the emigration of doctors, which is to be used to link loans and loans with the obligation to work them off in Poland. The authors propose both to modify existing student loans, which should actually make them more accessible, even by establishing a fixed margin of student loans at a maximum of 2% , as well as the support from the State budget of those who undertake to take medical studies for consideration in order to oblige them to subsequently work off loans in entities which provide public-funded healthcare services. They want a loan for med school. Such a loan will be an opportunity for people who have passed their high school exams, but who have not been able to study in a fixed-mode medical course. Often these people are unable to study for payment because of insufficient funding. Indeed, such a loan for medical studies can result in a significant increase in the number of students in a permanent manner. After all, there is an annual availability of permanent studies, and the number of these places can be significantly increased. Of course, we should ask ourselves whether these measures will significantly hinder the flow of young doctors. Without a strong qualitative change in the financing of health care, without raising the wages of residents, without making it easier for them to obtain specialized places they will leave Poland anyway. The risk of repayment of the borrowing will likely be calculated into job proposals received from employers from Western Europe. Nevertheless, we are inclined to support these ideas. However, another idea appeared in the draft law - the idea of extending the number of universities that can educate in the medical and dental fields to professional universities. The Act is intended to allow vocational universities to train in the courses assigned to them so far to studies with a general academic profile. Doctors could be trained by professional institutions which already conduct studies in at least one course of preparation for the medical profession, i.e. profession of nurse, midwife, physiotherapist, etc. The training of doctors requires, as we know, multi-directional academic staff, both multi-branch theorists and clinicists. It requires many studios, research facilities, laboratories, various equipment, but above all - solid clinical facilities. That's not going to be guaranteed by professional universities. The bill will lead to the creation of a doctor-like product. Unfortunately, the current idea is moving in this direction. Let us open new medical universities, help to expand already existing universities - the Minister said about it - let us increase recruitment for studies, encourage to study medicine and stay after graduation in Poland, let us support indeterminate studies, but let us not try (the bell) to take shortcuts. ParlaMint-PL_2021-10-01-sejm-38-3.u199	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I am pleased to present to the High House a draft law amending the Accounting Act and some other laws. First of all, it is worth mentioning that it is a long- consultative project, agreed not only on inter-ministerial cooperation, but also on a number of consultations with non-governmental bodies. It is largely a response to the demands of non-profit units. The aim of this project is above all to improve, simplify and make it easier for individuals to comply with their reporting obligations. With the proposed changes, individuals will also be able to use modern IT tools and users of reports will be able to quickly download, analyse and interpret the data they need. In addition, this law improves, gives additional competences of the Polish Audit Supervisory Agency. I would like to remind you that this is an entity that was created after the GetBack event when there were also deficiencies in the supervision of audit firms over financial market entities. The uprising and operation of the Lord is to prevent such events in the future. The most important change included in this project is the improvement of the signing of the reports by the members of the Management Board. The recent amendment meant that such reports had to be signed electronically by all members of the Management Board, which caused a number of problems for NGOs, including smaller towns, where the submission of such an electronic signature and the gathering of all representatives of the Management Board were problematic. This project is being amended here. From now on, with a multi-member board, an electronic signature will have to be made by one member of the Management Board, while the other members of the Management Board will be able to sign a report or refuse to sign a report by making a statement in the form of, inter alia, a statement. in writing, that is to say, a return to previous solutions. In this way, multi-member boards will be able to sign such reports more efficiently and will not be required of all such members to have a qualified electronic signature. The second major change that is being made in this project is the introduction of an obligation for issuers who are obliged to draw up financial statements in a uniform electronic standard format for the Member States of the European Union and this is so-called. ESEF format. For other entities applying international financial reporting standards, the Act introduces an obligation to apply the uniform reporting format of the ESFF or another searchable format when drawing up financial statements and business reports. This amendment will result in reports which have so far been often drawn up in a form which has prevented third parties from reading them, but also by members of the entity themselves, being in a uniform form, which will also improve transparency and their activities not only in the internal sphere but also in the external sphere. The third change proposed by the market, quite technical, but important for these entities, is the change concerning the consolidation of financial statements by alternative investment companies. Given the nature of ASI's activities, their obligation to draw up consolidated financial statements is not always justified. The amendment of the Accounting Act therefore proposes simplification in this respect. And the last group of changes are changes that I have already mentioned, i.e. changes in the activities of the Polish Audit Supervisory Agency which are largely related to the digitalisation of the activities of PAN. First of all, this is a clarification of the rules facilitating the holding of remote meetings and the adoption of resolutions on a circular basis by the board of agencies and bodies of the Polish Chamber of Auditors. Secondly, it is about enabling the agency to carry out remote controls in audit firms for all audits and introducing remote controls as a basic form of activity. Third, this concerns the possibility of dealing with matters relating to the operation of the list of audit firms by means of an electronic system. In addition, we facilitate communication between the agency and audit firms during the audit process, reduce the work intensity in preparing audit documents by using documents in electronic form rather than paper form, reduce the cost of audit on the part of both audit firms and agencies and reduce the time-consuming handling of cases. Finally, I would like to emphasise once again that the project was widely consulted. It responds to the demands of really many reporting entities, especially small and small NGOs. Therefore, I kindly ask the High House to adopt the draft law as proposed by the government. Thank you. [[Chairman of the proceedings includes Deputy Marshal Małgorzata Kidawa-Błońska Sejm]]

ParlaMint-PL_2021-10-01-sejm-38-3.u203	Mrs. Marshal! High Seym! Minister! I have the honour to present the position of the Parliamentary Club Civic Coalition on the government draft law amending the Accounting Act and some other laws, print no. 1570. The proposed law aims at adapting the current financial reporting and auditing rules to the current legal and economic situation and new technological opportunities. Proposals for changes are based on, among others, the provisions of Commission Delegated Regulation (EU) 2019/815 of 17 December 2018 supplementing Directive of the European Parliament and of the Council with regard to regulatory technical standards for the specifications of the uniform electronic reporting format. The draft law amends the following legal acts: the Accounting Act, the Corporate Income Tax Act, the Audit Act, audit firms and public oversight, and the Law on Specific Support Instruments in connection with the spread of SARS-CoV-2. The dynamic development of digital technologies also has a significant impact on accounting and financial revision. The possibility of using modern IT tools makes it easier for individuals to fulfil their accounting obligations and to draw up financial statements. Under Regulation 2019/815 issuers whose securities are admitted to trading on a regulated market are required to draw up their annual financial statements in a special format. Where an issuer's report contains consolidated financial statements drawn up in accordance with international accounting standards, such a report shall require special marking. This solution aims to increase the transparency, accessibility and comparability of the financial statements drawn up by listed companies and facilitate the analysis of those accounts. The draft shall also facilitate the signing of financial statements, in particular for bodies headed by a multi-member body. The obligation to sign reports by all members of a multi-member body to allow at least one member to do so shall be waived. The amendment to the Act on the National Court Register introduces the possibility to submit electronic copies of documents to the National Court Register - various kinds of statements - which in the original were made in paper form and then scanned. Numerous changes are made to the Act on statutory auditors, audit firms and public oversight. In the audit report, the statutory auditor shall give an opinion on the compliance of the audited report with the requirements of Regulation 2019/815. Provisions are also introduced concerning: the submission of an application for the change of data to be included in the list of audit firms and the procedures for its examination by the Agency; the submission of declarations of responsibility for the submission of false statements; the introduction of an electronic application for the listing of the audit firm or the deletion or amendment of it; questions of professional secrecy by the statutory auditor; other matters relating to the work of auditors which need to be regulated. The proposed amendments introduce into the Polish legal system regulations of EU law and other regulations, in particular concerning auditors, which need to be amended or clarified. To sum up, the Civic Coalition Club recommends that the bill be directed to further legislative work. Thank you for your attention.

We have to stop this. We therefore propose to increase the minimum rate of the mandate to PLN 1,500 for drivers exceeding the permissible speed by more than 30 km/h, and to PLN 3 000 in the course of a re-infringement within 2 years. zł. Driving after using alcohol will be fined with a minimum mandate of 2,5 thousand. zł. Of course, in duly justified cases, detention will be possible and the court will decide. Such a clear leap of penalties in these two areas must give rise to respect and must deter potential road pirates who threaten both themselves and others from violating the law. In our intention to increase the maximum amount of the fine issued in the mandate procedure to 5 000. PLN and up to 30,000. evil in judicial proceedings is a clear signal that for the most severe traffic offences there will be no mercy, no mercy. I stress the word "heaviest," i.e. those that directly endanger health or life. Those rates will not be covered by those who commit minor offences, e.g. wrong parking. I say this because in recent weeks, since this bill has been presented, various types of manipulations, contradictions and - you can say it in a language that is very popular in politics - fake news. From this place - and we always try to do it - I also want to correct so that these fake news does not appear anymore. We're not going to do that, we're not going to do that. Minor offences will not be subject to the amendment of the provisions of this law. On other issues, the project assumes an increase in the minimum rate also for: driving without permission, for riding abandoned dams and for not arriving on rail crossings, for obstructing or stopping traffic, for breaking the ban on overtaking, for not complying with sound signals. In addition to the point increase, the minimum mandates are being increased, and we are proposing changes to the system for the calculation of penalty points. The points will be cancelled after 2 years of receipt, but subject to a mandate fee for the traffic offences concerned. We also raise to 15 points the maximum dimension for the most dangerous offences. Other important changes concern the area of facilitating the possibility for close victims of road accidents to receive a pension. The families of road accident victims - traumatized, shocked - do not cope with the entire legal pension procedure. They will be able to receive support from your structures. We cannot leave such people alone in such difficult situations. The proposal therefore provides for the simplification of the pension procedure, which will be paid by the perpetrator of the intentional crime with the fatal effect of people nearest to the victim of a road accident who are dependent on the person who, as a result, lost the host. This benefit will be exempt from income tax and not included as income in the calculation of family benefits. Another important proposal is to depend on the amount of the insurance premium for motor vehicle owners on the number of points and the history of the most serious offences committed. The draft law provides for the provision of data from the CEPiK to insurance undertakings and allows for the adjustment of the rates of motor insurance to the history of the driver's offences and the points received by him. Will this also include punishing the police with multi-thousand seats of spontaneously protesting people? After all, this is an open way to violate civil liberties. I think that should be explained in the course of our work. According to the applicants, a drastic increase in penalties for offences is intended to build prevention and deter. But it's not the level of sanctions, it's its inevitability that builds prevention. The determination of penalties in total detachment from average earnings per head will make them unenforceable. According to the current rules, when execution is impossible, the fine can be converted into a limitation of freedom or detention for 30 days. It may turn out that penalties of 10, 15 or 30,000. Evil will be irresistible because the punished will not be able to pay them, which will result in their conversion into custody. What costs will the state budget bear in this way, not to mention social costs? Would it not be more appropriate to introduce the possibility of converting part of the penalty into social work? When we talk about the validity of the mandates, let us also consider how this affects the procedure for issuing mandates. The Ombudsman also raises doubts. A policeman within a few minutes without any deeper insight into the perp's earning opportunities and his personal situation can issue a ticket. even in the amount of 6,000. zł. Ba, can accept it in cash, which poses a real risk of corruption with such a disproportion of earnings. According to the project, the proceeds from penalties are to fund the National Road Fund for the construction and renovation of national roads and to improve their safety. However, the legislator does not define in any way what it means by improving safety and does not lean on the proportion of these funds. It is also doubtful that money should only go to national roads. Municipal roads are completely ignored, on which there are equally traffic offences and which also need funds for renovations and security infrastructure. In the light of the requests for assistance from victims of accidents, it is justified to request that part of the money be spent on a fund dedicated to victims of accidents which could provide the necessary financial assistance for them to live and recover, especially as victims of unintentional road traffic offences do not have their aid fund. In support of the victims, the project devotes a significant part of the project by proposing a dedicated course of pension proceedings for the nearest victims of an accident. However, the procedure relates only to intentional offences, and in the course of case law and practice it was assumed that road accidents are mostly involuntary offences. In addition, if we are talking about the pension procedure, it has several shortcomings. First of all, why do we support only one group of victims? What if the victim of an accident is permanently unable to work? [[♪ The bell ♪]] Your Marshal, may I extend by a dozen seconds? Yes. Thank you very much. Why should survivors be condemned to prove their injury and incapacity to work in long-term civil trials? Secondly, what appears to be the greatest advantage of the project, i.e. the initiation of proceedings with a copy of the final judgment, can clearly delay its initiation. After all, such appeals can take up to two years. In the ordinary security, the victims may apply for the payment of the pension already at the level of the winding-up procedure. In conclusion, I would like to say that the proposed act is a step in the right direction, but it needs to be amended. I believe that in the course of a fair and cross-party work in committees, without ignoring the views of legal institutions, social organisations and public hearing, we will create a legal act that will give justice to all victims who meet social expectations and will positively improve road safety. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2021-10-01-sejm-38-3.u319	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The Portuguese Presidency's time was another six months when the main determinant of the Union's action was the consequences of the pandemic. Luckily, not the most pressing problems directly related to medical, health or vaccine availability. These problems were solved, but most of all they were about economic consequences. The most important conclusion that the Union is trying to draw after a pandemic is, of course, the reduction of dependence on third countries in the area of trade policy, industrial policy. Poland was one of the promoters of such thinking, recognising that if the experience of pandemics is to bring something important in the Union, it is above all a reduction of dependence not only on medical issues but on digital issues, for example. In the area of building resilience, as the Presidency has called it, the debate on migration and asylum must certainly be seen. Poland has led to a breakthrough in European thinking in this area. We have indicated that any migration policy depends on whether we can effectively protect the external border. Today, under very dramatic circumstances, we find that we were right about this. The next issue is a block of climate issues. First of all, it should be noted that Poland, like all the countries of Central Europe, expects first and foremost flexibility in this policy, so that the pace, the transition scenario is adapted to our social and economic conditions. This is guaranteed by the fact that the transformation objectives are set at an EU-wide level. That gives us a field of flexibility. Also, 10-year settlement cycles offer quite a lot of flexibility for future decisions. However, decisions in the area of climate policy in the coming months will be the most difficult, most serious negotiations to which we will join. The main aim is to ensure that costs, burdens are distributed fairly both between countries and between sectors, as well as between social groups. Climate policy, energy and climate change must not be linked to an increase in energy poverty. At the same time, we stress that the countries of Central Europe, including Poland, must pursue an ambitious policy in this respect in their own interest, because delaying the energy transition results in only an increase in the costs of this process. In the digital area, the Portuguese Presidency focused on two main issues, two regulations. Firstly, in a market regulation, whose task is to lay down better rules on the relationship between large platforms and the rest of the market. The second point is the Digital Services Regulation, which in turn aims to improve the situation of users in particular with regard to the largest operators. In the social area, which was very important for Portugal, for the Portuguese Government, we had a social summit in Porto. Poland, when pursuing an extremely ambitious social policy, points out that the competences of the Member States play a key role in this matter, and all these documents, these declarations should not even suggest an extension of the European Union's legislative powers in the social area. The last aspect is global, international. We had two very important events. Firstly, the negative developments in Belarus, the wave of repression after the elections. This has led Poland to successfully mobilise the European opinion to adopt the fourth package of sanctions against Belarus, and to offer democratic Belarus, the future Belarus, real support, a real prospect of cooperation with Europe. This response made up of two components: a very strong opposition to the current regime and as far as possible openness to the future is, I think, a prerequisite for the success of this policy. The second point is politics towards Russia. These are closely related topics. The European Council, in line with Polish expectations, rejected the German-French proposals to return to talks with Russia at the highest political level. At the same time, we have confirmed our commitment to the key principles which should govern the European Union's policy towards Russia. Firstly, it is the implementation of Minsk agreements, respect for the integrity of Ukraine, support for civil society and maintain cooperation with the Russian Federation only at the lowest possible level, where both parties have a clear community of interests. The last element I would like to draw attention to is very small, very unsatisfactory progress, but nevertheless progress, in the area of enlargement policy, in particular towards the Western Balkans. We have adopted a new enlargement methodology for Serbia and Montenegro. Two intergovernmental conferences were held. Unfortunately, the same has not been done in relation to Albania and North Macedonia. These are elements of the European Union's policy that are incompatible with the European interest. It is in the European interest to pursue an active enlargement policy, an active policy in the Western Balkans region. We are very much talking about this, we are repeating this, in particular before the next informal summit with the Western Balkans next week. To conclude, I would like to say that in the international area, this Presidency has been focused on very important issues, particularly in relation to Belarus and Russia. These are key issues also from a Polish point of view. As far as economic issues are concerned, there has been progress, above all, in the European response to the economic consequences of the pandemic, in particular in the area of reducing dependence on third countries. I would like to ask the High Chamber to adopt information on Polish policy in the European Union during the Portuguese Presidency, which is much more detailed than my speech. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-PL_2021-10-01-sejm-38-3.u320	Thank you, Minister. I ask Mr Andrzej Grzyb to present the justification for the draft resolution.
ParlaMint-PL_2021-10-01-sejm-38-3.u321	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I submit on behalf of the European Union Commission a draft resolution in which the committee asks the High Sejm to accept the information submitted by the Government on the Polish activity during the Portuguese presidency of the Council of the European Union. This is the conclusion after the debate and this was the vote in the European Union Committee on Affairs. The Presidency took place at a time of very important changes in the international environment. First of all, there has been a change in the chair of the President of the United States. This has also opened up a new opportunity for the EU-US summit, which seems to open up a new era in relations between the European Union and the United States. In addition, we have faced this post-election crisis in Belarus and the sanction system that has been launched. I will not go further, because Minister Szymański spoke about it. But what was most important, of course, what all the Member States and all the EU institutions were most interested in, of course, is the pandemic or post-pandemic crisis. It took most of the time. Of course, in this environment it should also be mentioned that migration pressures have not been dealt with in any way, particularly on the African continent, in some way. No new instruments have been found in these relations to help anything, because this EU-Africa summit simply did not take place during the Presidency, and this was one of the priorities for the global Europe of the Presidency. It is also a failure to make further progress in terms of the Mercosur agreement. Of course, we have some reservations about the EU-Mercosur agreement. This is one of these elements. A resilient, digital, green, social and global Europe - such were the general calls that the Portuguese Presidency set up during its six-month presidency in the European Union. On 20 January, Portuguese Prime Minister António Costa presented the Presidency's programme in Parliament. He told me about the main targets. Rebuilding the economy after the pandemic. Vaccinations against COVID-19, but this is still in this context the full supply of vaccines to all Member States. It seems to me that there was also a question of helping third countries, because the European Union is one of the most important donors to fund precisely these vaccines for third countries. Without a global solution to this issue, and particularly in the European Union's neighbourhood, it will be difficult to talk about some important opening. And, of course, the digital and climate transformation and the strengthening of the pillar of social rights and relations with the United States and the United Kingdom, Great Britain, which is already outside the European Union. At the same time, he called for cooperation to achieve these objectives. The call was addressed not only to the European Parliament and the Commission but also to all Member States. Lapidarically, he included this in his statement, which was the Presidency's motto: It is time to act for a fair, ecological and digital recovery. These objectives were also presented in the Commission for European Union Affairs by the Portuguese Ambassador. One of the most important events of the Presidency, as also emphasised in his presentation in the High House by Minister Szymański, was the social summit, which was the main undertaking on social issues by the Portuguese Presidency. It was held in Porto on May 7th and 8th. And here we should add that this summit was not only about social issues in a literal sense, but also about the epidemiological situation and the digital, ecological and fair transformation of Europe. In particular, the Declaration identifies the issue of job protection and the role of small and medium-sized enterprises in this process, and the issue of social poverty, particularly among the weakest, that, so to speak, part of the European Union's societies, which are incomplete families and children. Member States, including Poland, at the Porto Summit accepted the action plan, which in fact approved the agenda that was adopted under the European Pillar of Social Rights, which was still adopted during the Swedish Presidency. An important element was the findings - and the debate on this - of the Community's agricultural policy for 20232027, which were finalised in the framework of the long-standing negotiations which have lasted since the previous parliamentary term and have been finalised by the Council and the European Parliament on 28 June 2021. This agreement was in fact expected already in 2018, as Mr Siekierski can testify, who chaired the Committee on Agriculture of the European Parliament in his previous term of office, but there was no political will either by the Member States or in Parliament to approve this package of regulations on Community agricultural policy. This has now been adopted, but this has delayed the implementation of the new agricultural policy, which has been postponed to 2023. That is why I stress this element of the Presidency's work, because the agreement has de facto defined fundamental reforms within the framework of the CAP, including. concerns the introduction of Community agricultural policy strategic plans. At the moment these works are taking place, among others. in the Polish government, there was also the first round of public consultation. The public consultation is translated into the provisions of the CAP Strategic Plan, which will have to be approved by the European Commission, as well as the National Plan for Reconstruction. So this is completely new. The second element is the common horizontal organisation of agricultural markets. In addition, climate law, which sets medium-term targets by 2030, was approved on 28 June, called Fit for 55. This concerns the reduction of net emissions by 2030 for 1990 and the achievement of climate neutrality by 2050. We will still have much to do with this package, because this will be followed by the whole legislative package that will be submitted this mid-year. The ratification of the Reconstruction Fund was also a success. We have had an emotional discussion in this House. This ratification has, of course, opened up the whole process of approval by the European Commission of national reconstruction programmes and, as such, is being validated by the Council of the European Union, i.e. the process of twice the approval of national plans has been launched. In this regard, Poland is still ahead of this decision. What was Poland's activity in priority areas? Minister Szymański spoke about this, but let me draw attention to the fact that in terms of Europe's resilience, Polish activity concerned among others. to participate in the development of border protection mechanisms, including those related to epidemic restrictions, with the so-called 'Border Protection Mechanisms'. a green passport, as well as what we would call exclusion from the introduction of a green passport, issues relating to the MFF, the multiannual financial framework, the Next Generation EU Fund, the verification of own resources, the Fund for Reconstruction and the SURE instrument aimed at financing the reduction of the risks associated with unemployment caused by the post-epidemic crisis. With regard to green Europe, the main priority was, of course, the discussion on climate neutrality issues, which has been repeatedly present in our House, or the future legislative package related to the Fit for task mentioned earlier. 55, which aims to build an architecture for the implementation of this objective by 2030. I think we will have a very broad and diverse discussion on these objectives. And, of course, I will mention here the common agricultural policy, which I was talking about initially. With regard to digital Europe, I would like to point out data management and to start working on the Digital Services Act and the regulation of digital markets on how fair relations between digital platforms and the services they use should develop, while ensuring the openness and transparency of these relations. Of course, I'll name it here. also issues concerning artificial intelligence, what rights to give artificial intelligence - as discussed in the previous term of the European Parliament - who will respond if artificial intelligence has negative effects: the owner of artificial intelligence or she herself will have legal responsibility, so to speak. These are insanely complicated matters. I said about social Europe. I would like to mention for the sake of order this - each time we talk about it - what financial transfers were. ParlaMint-PL_2021-10-01-sejm-38-3.u365	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Glass Piotr's. When entering Piotrków Trybunalski, the former line of the Warsaw-Vienna Railway from Koluszek's side, a careful traveler will notice a powerful industrial building. It is the remains of a large complex of industrial glassworks with quite exotic names: Kara and Hortensia. The first one has always produced clearly industrial assortment: window glass, which over time became its main product, but also bottles of various types, lighting cylinders and many others. It worked since 1897 and until 1935 all products were made by hand. Only then was the first glass pane pulling machine started. Kara was in foreign hands very often the arena of workers' strikes. Many steel workers volunteered to join the army in 1920 to defend their fresh independence from the Soviet crisis. During World War II, the area of Kara was a work camp for Jews from the Piotrków ghetto. It should be mentioned here that the Piotrków ghetto was the first ghetto in Europe and in the world and started its activities in the first days of September 1939. The Economic Glassworks of Kara, which did not survive the economic changes in the early 1990s, did not survive. While Kara produced industrial elements, Hortensia became a place for which art was born, not without a fennel. Artistic products from the Piotrkow steelworks delighted and still delight ordinary people and collectors. Very often at various auctions in Poland and abroad they reach high prices. Vases, plates from Hortensia are sought. The whole family had been involved with the plant for decades. They are known in Piotrków as the dynasty of steel workers who worked in it throughout its lifetime. During the thriving steelworks, Piotrków glass was sold to many countries on several continents. In 1989, a book on the history of the steelworks was published. This steelworks survived only a few years longer. Why am I talking about these bets today? It was from them that the real industrial development of Piotrków Trybunalski began, which was then the capital of the province, but practically there was no industry. Today the hut is gone, but the inhabitants of the city still remember about the Piotrków steelworks. Several small companies were built on their ruins. And when someone mentions this location, they say, "Here was Kara and there was Hortensia." Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2021-10-01-sejm-38-3.u367	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Social construction societies operate under the Act of 26 October 1995 on certain forms of support for construction. Their aim was primarily to build apartments for rent for people whose income was high enough that they could not benefit from the housing aid of the local government and at the same time were too low for them to take credit for their own apartment. TBS received financial support from the National Housing Fund, but it turns out that the control of TBS is limited because the Supreme Audit Office can only control TBSs whose shareholders are local government units, and that is only in so-called areas. the discipline of public finances. This means that the issue of TBS's disposal of funds from tenants, namely actually participants, is beyond all control of bodies not connected with the functioning of the social housing society. Another issue is TBS's ownership of participations paid by those interested in hiring companies. De facto, those who participated in the construction of the largest number of TBS in the country between 27 November 1995 and 31 December 2004 were deprived of the protection of their rights by the legislator. Despite the payment of between 25% and 30% of the cost of building a given housing unit, the participant obtained in return only the right to indicate the tenant of the premises, which in the vast majority of cases after a certain period of time laid down in the participating agreement simply expires. As a result of this legal solution, some TBSs deprived participants of the right to obtain reimbursement of paid participation in the event of termination of the agreement. TBSs often freely have paid-in participations without informing the participant either of their purpose or of their progress in the repayment of the loan. However, TBS is obliged to calculate the rent in such a way that its amount is sufficient in a given month only for the payment of the loan received, the cost of operating the building and the cost of renovations. If TBS collects from the tenants the funds to repay the loan and then does not transfer them to the bank by embezzling them in fact, the repayment period is extended by several years and the amount to be repaid will continue to fall on the premises concerned. TBS has no right to charge tenants several times with the same loan. The rent after repayment of the loan should be reduced by the amount of the instalment. Unfortunately, TBSs are not willing to provide tenants with any information on this subject. The tenants do not know often or for what period the TBS has incurred the loan and when the repayment should be completed on a scheduled basis and thus reduce the rent, or whether TBS has extended the repayment period due to a record of default with payment. Unfortunately, for many years TBS has been operating in some of them, there have been various kinds of pathologies and abuses, such as funding from money paid by music concert participants. Since the State grants public loans to entities such as TBS under preferential conditions, it should have control over how the agreements with the participating parties are constructed. From a legal point of view, participation is a property right and is inherited. The remaining value of the tenant's premises is paid in rent. This fact is of major importance, since it means that the repayment of the loan made by TBS is de facto not the borrower in its entirety but the tenant, or participant. This means that the tenant is burdened with the full cost of paying off the building and will never be the owner of the apartment. In other words, a social group was forced to choose TBS to have a place to live with their families because of insufficient income to buy the apartment. Meanwhile, the legislator has so unfortunately formulated legislation that this limited-income social group is forced to fund the municipality or private entity as the owner of TBS rental premises. The TBS Act must be amended and changes should move towards greater transparency and facilitation of the ownership of participants. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2021-10-01-sejm-38-3.u373	Thank you very much. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! In Poland it was assumed that the older person was a person aged 60 and over. In recent years, there has been an increasing process of population ageing in our country. The number of older people is growing systematically. At the end of 2019, the number of people aged 60 and over exceeded 9 million and increased by 2.1% compared to the previous year. The proportion of older people in the Polish population reached 25.3%. At the end of 2019, the number of people working at the age of 60 and over and having a fixed pension right amounted to 503 000. Compared to 2018, the number of these people increased by 7.5%. In 2019 the average monthly pension and gross pension paid from non-agricultural social security systems amounted to PLN 2327 gross. I stress: gross. The pension is deducted from the tax and health contribution, which will be increased in the near future and will absorb the proposed tax credit. Definitely lower was the average monthly pension and gross pension of individual farmers, which in 2019 reached just PLN 1292. It's gotten a little bigger lately. Progressive changes in the demographic structure of the population linked to the increase in the participation of older people represent a challenge for the economy and society. The ageing of the population has a major impact on the functioning of the health system - older people are more likely to go to doctors and benefit from social protection. And here, ladies and gentlemen, an even bigger problem begins. A few years ago, especially in rural areas, we had multigenerational families: grandparents, children and grandchildren. Grandparents took care of their grandchildren, the children worked, and everyone supported each other. This was conditioned, among others. no housing in the market. Today, improving the housing market, access to credit allows young people to quickly become independent and live on their own. Grandparents, older parents remain alone in their homes or apartments. As long as they're fit and self-sufficient, it's good, but the problem starts when they get sick or get old. We need 24-hour help a day. The care of the elderly in Poland is focused on social assistance homes, which are part of the public system. The care there is on very different levels, the rooms are multiplayer and it is more associated with hospice than a nursing home for seniors. Insufficient staff, lack of space, not enough rehabilitation. Social assistance homes, state units account for 43% the total number of long-term residences of older people and there are more than 60% of beds. This means that these are mostly large facilities. Size in this case does not always go hand in hand with the quality of services. The number of institutions where patients have to pay for their stay from their own pockets rather than through the National Fund or pensions is growing dynamically in recent years. There's a lot of demand for private nursing homes for seniors. Thanks to this, they get among others. smaller room or numerous amenities and services, e.g. relaxation and beauty zones, married apartments decorated in the form of apartments. The dynamic growth of private institutions in recent years mainly concerns so-called. 24-hour care facilities, which represent the majority of nearly 700 private institutions currently operating in Poland. It is also worth noting that a large number of people staying in the DPS will never become clients of private establishments due to the limited financial resources they have at their disposal. The average price of a person staying in a senior's private house depending on the quality of services varies: from 4 thousand. PLN per month, goes up, some are up to 12 thousand. zł. Sometimes the whole family does, but what if the family is small or missing? Believe me, for a Polish senior who worked hard for several decades, the use of family help is humiliating. [[♪ The bell ♪]] Polish seniors don't want to cause the family any trouble. Thank you very much.
