ParlaMint-PL_2021-10-01-sejm-38-3.u385	Declaration on Cyprian Kamil Norwid for his 200th anniversary. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Together with colleagues from the parliamentary benches last year, we adopted a resolution to establish the year 2021 as the year of the great artist, poet and prose writer Cyprian Kamil Norwid. September 24th was the 200th anniversary of his birthday. He was uncompromising in the evaluations of the modern world, which caused that only part of his works was published in life, from poetry it was only one volume of poems. The most important thing for Norwid was patriotism and deep Catholic faith. In his work he focused primarily on man. He spent his youth in Poland, while he was an adult in emigration, from which he could not return due to the recognition of him as a political exile by the Russian authorities. He died on May 23, 1883, forgotten in a Paris workhouse, leaving behind wonderful works - both literary and painting. The Renaissance of Norwid's works began only during the Young Poland period, when poems of the poet came to the hands of Zenon Przesmycki. Today Cyprian Kamil Norwid is the patron of many institutions or places. His paintings and graphics are wanted by collectors, and literary works are subjected to continuous analysis and interpretation by many artists of the contemporary artistic scene. Thank you for your attention.
ParlaMint-PL_2021-10-01-sejm-38-3.u386	Declaration on the European Day for Combating Depression. In my statement, I would like to draw attention to depression as a disease with which more and more people are struggling, including in Poland. On 1 October, there is a European Day for Combating Depression, but we should remember this illness and illness every day, as depression is the fourth most serious disease in the world and one of the main causes of suicide. According to current estimates, it is assumed that by 2030 it will also become the first most frequently diagnosed disease unit in the world. There are 350 million people in the world and 4 million in Poland. Today, as well as every day, we should remember that depression is not a sign of weakness. It can touch everyone, regardless of age, gender or social status. We must remember that this condition reduces mood and makes everyday functioning difficult or impossible. The result is anxiety, sadness, lack of energy and very low self-esteem. Depression takes away the daily joy of life and contributes to severe mental suffering, and its failure to cure it may later have serious consequences, including the taking of a life of its own. Let us remember that support for loved ones is one of the first and key factors in the process of mental recovery. Depression can be completely cured by pharmacological treatment and therapy with a psychologist or a psychotherapist. Not only today, but every day let's pay attention to our loved ones and the people around us, and then help them get back in shape.
ParlaMint-PL_2021-10-01-sejm-38-3.u387	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! On Friday, September 17th, we celebrate World Sybirak Day. This important celebration was established on the anniversary of the Russian attack on Poland and although the celebrations organized by the Sibirak Union have been held annually since 1991, it was only in 2013 that the Polish Sejm formally established the day of 17 September Sibirak. History has many ways of persecuting the population because of nationality, social status, or religion. Concentration camps, mass executions, or eventually alienated fundamental human rights, stripped humanity of humanity. These actions are an expression of the darkest pages of history. Their effect was not only to humiliate the victims, but also to glorify the power of the oppressors. This was the fate of the Sibiraks, who were massively exported into the cold guts of the USSR and to the endless steppes of Kazakhstan. Crammed in cattle cars, decimated by disease and frost. Their bones still lie in unknown graves by roads and tracks, in forests and on broken steppe land. At this point it should be emphasized that our fellow countrymen in Siberia began in the 17th century. The first group of exiles was about 10,000. participants in the bar confederation. In the following centuries, insurgents who participated in all independence breaks, in the uprisings of Kościuszko, November and January were sent to Russia. The turn of the 19th and 20th centuries is the time of the troops of independence conspiracy activists, including General Józef Piłsudski. The blackest part of the story concerning the exiles of Poles to the Far East began with the Soviet aggression in September 1939. After September 17, over 1.5 million Poles were deported to Siberia and Kazakhstan. Sibir consumed many lives, hundreds of thousands of victims were not allowed to return to their homeland. Those who have succeeded in this are still remembering the humiliating conditions in which they live. The exile left a deep scar in the consciousness of all who came to experience it. Although many years have passed since those tragic events, the memory of the harm that has been done is still intact. Today, the last sibiracs are slowly leaving, along with them memories and stories of inhuman events that occurred to them on distant eastern earth. September 17 is a special date in the history of Poland. It is marked by the great pain and suffering of millions of Poles. Let us remember that this day has a unique dimension especially for sibiracs and the vernaculars. It is a symbol of the marking of their lives, and at the same time a meaningful way to preserve in the memory of their tragedy and wandering fate. Let us make every effort to ensure that the dramatic events of this period survive in the memory and consciousness of subsequent generations. Therefore, let us devote this day to meetings with former sibiracs to listen to their difficult stories and then be able to pass them on to the next generation. Thank you for your attention.
